# [Perf] imports: Keep light modules off HF patches, model configs, and the scheduler

[Upstream PR](https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/47731) · state: closed · merged: True

## Original PR description

- `sglang/__init__.py` no longer applies the transformers compatibility patches (`apply_all`, renamed `apply_all_hf_patches`). They are applied by the HF loading layer (`sglang.srt.utils.hf_transformers`) on import, and by the code that loads HF objects straight from transformers: `sglang.benchmark.utils`, `sglang.test.test_utils`, `one_batch_server`, and the comparator.
- The `LlamaFlashAttention2` re-export now lands when `transformers.models.llama.modeling_llama` is imported (a `sys.meta_path` hook), instead of importing that module and its modeling/quantizer chain up front. That import was 2.6s of the 3.3s the patches cost.
- Drop `_patch_flash_attn_availability`: transformers 5.19 already excludes the `flash-attn-4` distribution in `is_flash_attn_2_available`, so the patch is a no-op. Also drop the stale `PilImageProcessingMixin` name, the redundant mistral-patch flag, and a duplicate `ForwardMode.is_dllm_extend`.
- `sglang/srt/configs/__init__.py` is empty. Its aggregate import moved to `sglang.srt.configs.all_configs`, which the HF loading layer uses for `_CONFIG_REGISTRY` and the AutoConfig/AutoProcessor registrations. The move is byte for byte except that `Cohere2MoeConfig` joins `__all__`: it is imported only to register itself with transformers, and outside a package `__init__` the linter would drop the import as unused. Other importers take each config from its submodule.
- `ModelConfig._verify_quantization` imports the quantization registry when it runs, so importing `model_config` no longer loads every quantization method's kernels.
- Light types split out of heavy modules, moved byte for byte; every importer now imports them from the new module:
  - `ForwardMode`, `CaptureHiddenMode` -> `sglang.srt.model_executor.forward_mode` (from `forward_batch_info`)
  - `DisaggregationMode`, `FAKE_BOOTSTRAP_HOST` -> `sglang.srt.disaggregation.mode` (from `disaggregation.utils`)
  - `MultimodalDataItem`, `MultimodalProcessorOutput`, `ReturnHiddenStatesMode`, `SamplingLogprobsMode`, `get_return_hidden_states_mode`, `MM_PAD_SHIFT_VALUE` -> `sglang.srt.managers.req_types` (from `schedule_batch`)
- `schedule_batch` imports `DllmConfig` only under `TYPE_CHECKING`.
- `logging.Logger.warning_once` / `info_once`, which many modules call, came from transformers patching `logging.Logger` on import. `sglang` now installs them itself (`sglang/_logging_once.py`, same once-per-message behavior), keeping transformers' versions if it was imported first.

No behavior change, except: `import sglang` alone no longer patches transformers; `from sglang.srt.configs import XConfig` must import from the submodule (or `all_configs`); and `schedule_batch` no longer re-exports `MultimodalInputFormat`, `CudaIpcTensorTransportProxy`, or `flatten_nested_list`.


Test and CI changes, needed because importing `sglang` or `test_utils` no longer loads transformers, torch inductor, and the quantization registry up front:

- The CPU stage's fork worker preloads `transformers.models.llama.modeling_llama` and `sglang.srt.layers.quantization`, which `test_utils` no longer pulls in, so each child does not import them again. On a host with a CUDA driver, the worker skips extra preloads and checks CUDA availability through NVML: a child forked after the parent called `cuInit` or loaded flashinfer or sgl_kernel crashes on its first driver call.
- Three tests imported real modules inside a module-level `patch.dict(sys.modules)` stub (`test_w4afp8_deepep_post_reorder.py`, `test_encoder_decoder_varlen_gather.py`, `test_mem_pool_ep_unit.py`). The stub unloads every module first imported inside it while torch keeps their registrations, so a later import failed once `import sglang` stopped loading that stack up front. Each now loads its real dependency before the stub.

Import time at 51a3f5f7f4 against its merge base on main (e152f531d9), CPU only, warm `.pyc`, median of 3:

| module | main (s) | this PR (s) |
|---|---|---|
| `sglang`, `sglang.srt.environ` | 4.8 | 0.25 |
| `sglang.cli.main` | 4.7 | 0.29 |
| `sglang.test.ci.ci_register` | 4.8 | 0.27 |
| `sglang.srt.server_args`, `sglang.launch_server` | 4.9 | 1.6 |
| `sglang.srt.managers.io_struct` | 7.9 | 1.7 |
| `sglang.srt.entrypoints.openai.protocol` | 5.1 | 2.8 |
| `sglang.test.test_utils` | 7.6 | 4.9 |
| `sglang.srt.entrypoints.engine`, `scheduler` | 8.6 | 8.5-8.6 |
| `sglang.srt.managers.tokenizer_manager`, `detokenizer_manager` | 8.0 | 8.2-8.3 |

The heavy entrypoints load the same modules as on main. The last row's 0.2-0.3s (median of 7) is one extra full (generation 2) garbage collection during the import, triggered by the changed allocation order: with `gc.disable()`, `tokenizer_manager` imports in 7.07s on main and 7.15s here.

Each of the 306 changed modules imports with the same result as on main.
